self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n416\t    ) -> None:\n417\t        \"\"\"``parallel=16`` saturates to 15; no worker observes a 16th peer.\"\"\"\n418\t\n419\t        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n420\t        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n421\t        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n422\t        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n423\t\n424\t        # Use a hold-event to keep every worker alive until the test\n425\t        # releases them, so the concurrency snapshot has a chance to\n426\t        # observe   concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n        )\n\n        specs = _build_specs(15)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n        outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=8)\n\n        for outcome in outcomes:\n            isolation = isolations_seen[outcome.eval_id]\n            event = _read_telemetry_event(isolation)\n            assert event[\"eval_id\"] == outcome.eval_id, (\n                f\"Cross-talk: telemetry for {outcome.eval_id} reads \"\n                f\"{event['eval_id']!r}\"\n            )\n            assert event[\"session_id\"] == isolation.session_id\n            # The env() snapshot baked into the JSONL agrees with the\n            # canonical record values — closes the bug where ``env()``\n            # would silently drop CLAUDE_SESSION_ID under concurrency.\n            assert event[\"env_session_id\"] == isolation.session_id\n            assert event[\"env_home\"] == event[\"home_path\"]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC: parallel=16 clamps to MAX_PARALLEL=15\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestParallelClampAtFifteen:\n    def test_parallel_sixteen_clamps_to_fifteen(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``parallel=16`` saturates to 15; no worker observes a 16th peer.\"\"\"\n\n        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n        # Use a hold-event to keep every worker alive until the test\n        # releases them, so the concurrency snapshot has a chance to\n        # observe the upper-bound peer count. Without the barrier the\n        # workers complete fast enough that the snapshot under-counts.\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=eval_config,\n            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n            hold_event=hold_event,\n        )\n\n        # Submit 20 specs at parallel=16; if the clamp leaks we'll\n        # observe more than 15 workers running concurrently. Once the\n        # peak is observed we release the barrier so the pool drains.\n        specs = _build_specs(20)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n\n        # Release the barrier on a delay so workers actually pile up\n        # before any of them finish. The delay is generous (1s) but\n        # bounded so a hung worker cannot block the test indefinitely.\n        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=16)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()  # unconditionally release in case of failure\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        assert len(outcomes) == 20\n        # The clamp is the *only* thing that can keep observed_concurrency\n        # bounded at 15: ``ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=16)`` would\n        # have admitted a 16th worker before any of them finished.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) <= RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL, (\n            f\"observed concurrency peaked at {max(observed_concurrency)} \"\n            f\"workers; clamp at {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL} leaked\"\n        )\n        # Sanity check that the test actually achieved parallelism — if\n        # the snapshot peaks at 1 the assertion above is vacuous.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) > 1, (\n            \"concurrency snapshot is degenerate; clamp assertion is vacuous\"\n        )\n\n    def test_parallel_at_max_admits_fifteen_concurrent_workers(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``parallel=15`` actually admits all 15 workers simultaneously.\"\"\"\n\n        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=eval_config,\n            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n            hold_event=hold_event,\n        )\n\n        specs = _build_specs(15)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=15)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        assert len(outcomes) == 15\n        # Confirms the clamp does not under-admit either: when the\n        # request equals MAX_PARALLEL, all 15 workers should run\n        # concurrently. Without the barrier the snapshot could\n        # under-count by chance; with the 1s hold every spawned worker\n        # is parked in ``hold_event.wait`` long enough to be counted.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) == RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL, (\n            f\"parallel=15 admitted only {max(observed_concurrency)} \"\n            f\"concurrent workers; expected {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL}\"\n        )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _read_telemetry_home(iso: HomeIsolation) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the ``home_path`` stamp the worker baked into the JSONL log.\n\n    ``HomeIsolation.teardown(keep=True)`` clears the private ``_home_path``\n    slot so :attr:`HomeIsolation.home_path` raises ``RuntimeError`` after\n    the worker returns. Reading the value from disk closes that gap\n    *and* doubles as a verification that the JSONL was actually written\n    by the worker we expected.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return _read_telemetry_event(iso)[\"home_path\"]\n\n\ndef _read_telemetry_event(iso: HomeIsolation) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Parse the eval's ``telemetry.jsonl`` and return the first event.\n\n    The file is written exactly once per eval, so the first JSON object\n    is the canonical record of what the worker observed when ``setup``\n    returned.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # The worker's JSONL lives at ``home_path/.eval-logs/telemetry.jsonl``;\n    # we cannot ask ``iso.home_path`` because teardown(keep=True) cleared\n    # the slot. Instead re-derive the path by scanning ``home_root`` for\n    # the subdir whose name starts with the eval id (``mkdtemp`` prefix\n    # convention in HomeIsolation.setup).\n    home_root = iso.home_root\n    candidates = [\n        p\n        for p in home_root.iterdir()\n        if p.is_dir() and p.name.startswith(f\"{iso.eval_id}-\")\n    ]\n    assert len(candidates) == 1, (\n        f\"expected exactly one preserved HOME for {iso.eval_id} under \"\n        f\"{home_root}; found {[str(c) for c in candidates]}\"\n    )\n    telemetry_path = candidates[0] / \".eval-logs\" / \"telemetry.jsonl\"\n    assert telemetry_path.exists(), (\n        f\"telemetry log missing for {iso.eval_id} at {telemetry_path}\"\n    )\n    with telemetry_path.open(encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n        return json.loads(fh.readline())\n","numLines":560,"startLine":1,"totalLines":560}}}

The\n  full containment check on ``suffix`` lands in T02.08; this method\n  performs the minimum necessary check that the joined path stays under\n  ``home_path``.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* DM-006 record spec (roadmap row 26).\n* COMP-006 method surface (roadmap rows 28 / 32; T02.07 / T02.11).\n* FR-SCH2 :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n* FR-ISO2 ``containment_guard`` (T02.08) — strengthens the symlink /\n  allowlist / regex checks layered on top of :meth:`setup` and\n  :meth:`state_path`.\n* DOC-OQ8 (T06.03) — gates ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` semantics.\n* COMP-012 probe (T02.05 — ``test_isolation_layers_probe.py``) — pins the\n  upstream ``IsolationLayers`` shape this module sits beside.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nimport tempfile\nimport traceback\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom .config import EvalConfig, ScratchRootViolation, resolve_scratch_root\nfrom .loader import InvalidEvalId, validate_eval_id\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\",\n    \"HomeContainmentViolation\",\n    \"HomeIsolation\",\n    \"containment_guard\",\n]\n\n\n# NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract — see ``HomeIsolation.setup`` docstring and\n# D-0033/spec.md. An\n177\t        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n178\t        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n179\t        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n180\t        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n181\t            raise TypeError(\n182\t                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n183\t            )\n184\t        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n185\t            raise ValueError(\n186\t                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n187\t            )\n188\t        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n189\t\n190\t        if not specs:\n191\t            return []\n192\t\n193\t        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n194\t        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n195\t        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n196\t        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n197\t\n198\t        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n199\t        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n247\t                # In-flight workers continue to completion even after a\n248\t                # disk-budget breach — the AC6 contract requires \"in-\n249\t                # Production wiring composes\n# ``EvalRunner.run`` with the per-spec resource allocation the\n# orchestrator deliberately does not own.\nEvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]\n\n\nclass RunOrchestrator:\n    \"\"\"Schedule per-spec eval workers in parallel via ``ThreadPoolExecutor``.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    run_one:\n        Worker callable invoked once per :class:`EvalSpec`. Returns the\n        :class:`EvalOutcome` for that spec. Worker exceptions are folded\n        into an ``ERRORED`` outcome — the orchestrator never re-raises\n        out of :meth:`run`.\n    cancellation_token:\n        Optional :class:`CancellationToken` shared with the CLI\n        dispatcher's signal handler (NFR-REL1 / T03.07). When the token\n        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n        never scheduled.\n    disk_budget_poller:\n        Optional :class:`DiskBudgetPoller` enforcing the NFR-PERF4\n        ``--max-disk-mb`` budget (T03.19 / D-0060 / R-060). When wired,\n        the orchestrator starts the poller before submission, checks\n        :meth:`DiskBudgetPoller.is_breached` alongside the cancellation\n        token between submissions, and synthesises a ``SKIPPED`` outcome\n        with ``skip_reason=\"disk_budget_exceeded\"`` for every spec that\n        was never scheduled after a breach. In-flight workers run to\n        completion — the poller never interrupts a thread. A disabled\n        poller (``max_disk_mb=0``) is silently no-op'd by\n        :meth:`DiskBudgetPoller.start`, so the orchestrator can be wired\n        with a poller unconditionally.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        run_one: EvalWorker,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n        disk_budget_poller: Optional[DiskBudgetPoller] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not callable(run_one):\n            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n        self._run_one = run_one\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n        self._disk_budget_poller = disk_budget_poller\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(\n        self,\n        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n        *,\n        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        specs:\n            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n            renders rows deterministically.\n        parallel:\n            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        list[EvalOutcome]\n            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. An\n        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n            )\n        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n\n        if not specs:\n            return []\n\n        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. They\n        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the\n        # disk-budget poller flipped its breach flag. They receive a\n        # synthesised ``SKIPPED`` outcome with ``skip_reason ==\n        # \"disk_budget_exceeded\"`` so the Reporter can distinguish a\n        # disk-budget abort from a SIGINT.\n        disk_budget_skipped_indices: list[int] = []\n\n        # The poller is started before the submission loop so it has at\n        # least one tick of head-start. It is shut down in a ``finally``\n        # so an unhandled exception in the pool body still releases the\n        # background thread.\n        poller = self._disk_budget_poller\n        if poller is not None:\n            poller.start()\n        try:\n            with ThreadPoolExecutor(\n                max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n            ) as pool:\n                futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n\n                for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n                    # Cooperative cancellation: once the token flips, stop\n                    # submitting new work. Already-running workers observe\n                    # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                    # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                    # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                    # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                    #\n                    # Cancellation is checked *before* the disk-budget\n                    # breach so a SIGINT received concurrently with a\n                    # breach routes the spec to INTERRUPTED (operator\n                    # intent dominates a resource limit).\n                    if self._is_cancelled():\n                        cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    if self._is_disk_budget_exceeded():\n                        disk_budget_skipped_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                    futures[future] = index\n\n                # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n                # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n                # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n                # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n                # In-flight workers continue to completion even after a\n                # disk-budget breach — the AC6 contract requires \"in-\n                # flight evals complete\" before the run exits.\n                for future in as_completed(futures):\n                    index = futures[future]\n                    spec = specs[index]\n                    try:\n                        outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                        # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                        # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                        # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                        # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                        # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                        # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                        outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n        finally:\n            if poller is not None:\n                poller.stop()\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n        # Backfill SKIPPED outcomes for specs we declined to submit\n        # because the disk budget was breached. Same post-shutdown\n        # placement as the INTERRUPTED backfill — once we are here the\n        # pool has drained, so the synthesised outcomes cannot race\n        # with an in-flight result.\n        for index in disk_budget_skipped_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._disk_budget_skipped_outcome(specs[index])\n\n        # Every slot must be populated by now. The cast filters the\n        # ``Optional[...]`` typing without runtime cost; the assertion\n        # guards against a future regression.\n        assert all(o is not None for o in outcomes), (\n            \"RunOrchestrator: one or more outcome slots were left empty\"\n        )\n        return [o for o in outcomes if o is not None]\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _invoke_worker(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Indirection so subclasses / tests can hook the call site.\"\"\"\n        return self._run_one(spec)\n\n    def _is_cancelled(self) -> bool:\n        token = self._cancellation_token\n        return token is not None and token.is_cancelled()\n\n    def _is_disk_budget_exceeded(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` when the wired poller has flagged a budget breach.\n\n        Returns ``False`` when no poller was wired or the poller is\n        still healthy. Mirrors :meth:`_is_cancelled` so the submission\n        loop reads naturally.\n        \"\"\"\n        poller = self._disk_budget_poller\n        return poller is not None and poller.is_breached()\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _disk_budget_skipped_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build a SKIPPED outcome for a spec that lost out to the disk budget.\n\n       return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"SKIPPED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON,\n            skip_flag_triggered=\"--max-disk-mb\",\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _interrupted_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build the synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for a skipped spec.\n\n        Mirrors :meth:`EvalRunner._make_interrupted_outcome` so the\n        reporter cannot distinguish an in-flight cancellation from an\n        orchestrator-level one — both shapes are byte-identical.\n        \"\"\"\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"INTERRUPTED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _errored_outcome(spec: EvalSpec, exc: BaseException) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Fold a worker exception into a DM-001 ``ERRORED`` outcome.\n\n        ``error_class`` is the fully-qualified exception classname so\n        the Reporter can group identical harness failures across runs\n        without parsing free-form messages. Matches the format\n        :func:`run_eval` uses for in-runner harness failures.\n        \"\"\"\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"ERRORED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n        )\n","numLines":295,"startLine":80,"totalLines":374}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Vt2E1Njodhyjk4QiqWkfU6","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0058 — Design Notes\n2\t\n3\tCompanion to `spec.md`. Captures the choices made while implementing\n4\tT03.16 (FR-G2: parallel-15 integration test) and the alternatives\n5\tconsidered.\n6\t\n7\t## Why an integration test rather than two unit tests\n8\t\n9\tThe orchestrator and isolation components each have dedicated unit\n10\tsuites (`test_orchestrator.py` 20 tests, `test_home_isolation.py` 27\n11\ttests). What neither suite proves is that they **compose correctly under\n12\tconcurrency**:\n13\t\n14\t- The orchestrator unit tests use trivial stub workers that never touch\n15\t  the filesystem.\n16\t- The isolation unit tests run lifecycle on a single instance at a time\n17\t  (with one explicit `TestParallelSiblings.test_parallel_lifecycle_round_trip`\n18\t  but only at width=2).\n19\t\n20\tFR-G2's contract is specifically about the *composition*: 15 isolations\n21\tchurning through a real `ThreadPoolExecutor` without colliding on HOME\n22\tpaths, sessions, or telemetry namespaces. That requires real\n23\t`HomeIsolation.setup` calls under the orchestrator's scheduling, which\n24\tis exactly the integration boundary D-0058 owns.\n25\t\n26\t## Why a worker factory instead of `HomeIsolation` inside `RunOrchestrator`\n27\t\n28\t`RunOrchestrator` takes an `EvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`\n29\texactly so the orchestrator does not have to know about HOME, session,\n30\tor telemetry layout (see D-0057 notes). T03.16 demonstrates that this\n31\tcontract is honoured: the test wires `HomeIsolation` into the worker at\n32\tthe call site, the orchestrator never references isolation types. If\n33\tthis composition broke, this test would catch it. The worker factory\n34\tpattern (`_make_isolation_worker(scratch_root, config, …)`) closes over\n35\tconfiguration so individual test cases can swap concurrency barriers\n36\twithout redefining the wiring.\n37\t\n38\t## How \"telemetry namespace\" was interpreted\n39\t\n40\tFR-G2 lists \"state/telemetry namespace\" alongside HOME and session_id\n41\tas a per-eval isolation surface, but the design spec doesn't pin a\n42\tspecific path. Two anchors made the choice unambiguous:\n43\t\n44\t1. `EvalContext.jsonl_paths` in `models.py` already documents\n45\t   `{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}` as the per-eval JSONL contract.\n46\t2. `HomeIsolation.state_path(suffix)` exists specifically to scope\n47\t   per-eval files under the eval's HOME (`home_path / suffix`).\n48\t\n49\tSo the namespace is realised as `home_path / \".eval-logs\" / \"telemetry.jsonl\"`.\n50\tTest 4 (`test_each_eval_has_isolated_telemetry_namespace`) asserts both\n51\t**uniqueness** (15 distinct namespaces) and **containment** (each\n52\tnamespace strictly under its own HOME). Test 5 verifies the contents\n53\tmatch the spec that owns the file, ruling out cross-talk.\n54\t\n55\t## Why read telemetry after teardown via `home_root` scan\n56\t\n57\t`HomeIsolation.teardown(keep=True)` preserves the directory but clears\n58\tthe `_home_path` slot, so the post-run inspection can't ask the\n59\tinstance \"what was your home_path?\". Two options:\n60\t\n61\t1. **Capture `home_path` during the worker run** and store it in the\n62\t   record alongside `session_id` etc. This is what\n63\t   `record[\"home_path\"]` already does, so most tests use this path.\n64\t2. **Scan `home_root` for `{eval_id}-*` dirs** to verify the\n65\t   `from-the-outside` view that mkdtemp's `prefix=` did the right thing.\n66\t   Helpers `_read_telemetry_event` / `_read_telemetry_home` use this so\n67\t   the namespace assertions don't depend on the worker's bookkeeping.\n68\t\n69\tBoth views are checked. They agree iff the prefix mkdtemp contract holds.\n70\t\n71\t## Why three views of `session_id` (record, JSONL, env)\n72\t\n73\tThe contract is \"each eval owns its own session_id.\" A passing test\n74\tthat reads only one view (e.g. the worker's record) wouldn't catch a\n75\tregression where `env()` returns a stale value while the record looks\n76\tright. So `test_each_eval_receives_unique_session_id` checks:\n77\t\n78\t- `record[\"session_id\"]` — what the worker captured at `env()` time.\n79\t- JSONL's `session_id` field — what was written to disk.\n80\t- `iso.env()[\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"]` — what a downstream caller would\n81\t  read *after* the run, before teardown clears the slot.\n82\t\n83\tWait — teardown clears the slot. The test therefore re-creates\n84\t`HomeIsolation` with the same args and calls `env()` post-run, which\n85\treturns the same `session_id` because `session_id` is a constructor\n86\tfield, not a mkdtemp output. The point is to prove that the env() view\n87\tmatches the disk view, which it does.\n88\t\n89\t(An earlier draft tried to read `env()` on the original instance, which\n90\traised `RuntimeError` after teardown. The current shape correctly\n91\ttreats `session_id` as a deterministic input and verifies all three\n92\tviews agree.)\n93\t\n94\t## Why two clamp tests instead of one\n95\t\n96\t`TestParallelClamp::test_parallel_above_max_clamps_to_fifteen` in\n97\tT03.15 already proves the clamp downward. What that test doesn't\n98\tprove is that the clamp is **not over-restrictive** — e.g. a regression\n99\tthat clamped everything to 10 would still pass that test (≤15 stays\n100\t≤15). `test_parallel_at_max_admits_fifteen_concurrent_workers` closes\n101\tthat gap: with parallel=15 and 15 specs holding a barrier, exactly 15\n102\tmust be in flight simultaneously. Together the two clamp tests pin both\n103\tends of the NFR-PERF2 contract.\n104\t\n105\t## Concurrency barrier design\n106\t\n107\tNaïve \"count active workers\" measurements race: by the time you read\n108\t`len(active)`, an earlier worker may have decremented. The pattern\n109\tused here:\n110\t\n111\t```\n112\tenter() {\n113\t  with lock: active.append(spec)\n114\t  with lock: observed_concurrency.append(len(active))\n115\t  if hold_event: hold_event.wait()\n116\t  with lock: active.remove(spec)\n117\t}\n118\t```\n119\t\n120\tThe snapshot is taken **under the lock**, immediately after appending,\n121\tso `max(observed_concurrency)` accurately reflects the peak. Captures the choices made while implementing\nT03.16 (FR-G2: parallel-15 integration test) and the alternatives\nconsidered.\n\n## Why an integration test rather than two unit tests\n\nThe orchestrator and isolation components each have dedicated unit\nsuites (`test_orchestrator.py` 20 tests, `test_home_isolation.py` 27\ntests). What neither suite proves is that they **compose correctly under\nconcurrency**:\n\n- The orchestrator unit tests use trivial stub workers that never touch\n  the filesystem.\n- The isolation unit tests run lifecycle on a single instance at a time\n  (with one explicit `TestParallelSiblings.test_parallel_lifecycle_round_trip`\n  but only at width=2).\n\nFR-G2's contract is specifically about the *composition*: 15 isolations\nchurning through a real `ThreadPoolExecutor` without colliding on HOME\npaths, sessions, or telemetry namespaces. That requires real\n`HomeIsolation.setup` calls under the orchestrator's scheduling, which\nis exactly the integration boundary D-0058 owns.\n\n## Why a worker factory instead of `HomeIsolation` inside `RunOrchestrator`\n\n`RunOrchestrator` takes an `EvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`\nexactly so the orchestrator does not have to know about HOME, session,\nor telemetry layout (see D-0057 notes). T03.16 demonstrates that this\ncontract is honoured: the test wires `HomeIsolation` into the worker at\nthe call site, the orchestrator never references isolation types. If\nthis composition broke, this test would catch it. The worker factory\npattern (`_make_isolation_worker(scratch_root, config, …)`) closes over\nconfiguration so individual test cases can swap concurrency barriers\nwithout redefining the wiring.\n\n## How \"telemetry namespace\" was interpreted\n\nFR-G2 lists \"state/telemetry namespace\" alongside HOME and session_id\nas a per-eval isolation surface, but the design spec doesn't pin a\nspecific path. Two anchors made the choice unambiguous:\n\n1. `EvalContext.jsonl_paths` in `models.py` already documents\n   `{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}` as the per-eval JSONL contract.\n2. `HomeIsolation.state_path(suffix)` exists specifically to scope\n   per-eval files under the eval's HOME (`home_path / suffix`).\n\nSo the namespace is realised as `home_path / \".eval-logs\" / \"telemetry.jsonl\"`.\nTest 4 (`test_each_eval_has_isolated_telemetry_namespace`) asserts both\n**uniqueness** (15 distinct namespaces) and **containment** (each\nnamespace strictly under its own HOME). Test 5 verifies the contents\nmatch the spec that owns the file, ruling out cross-talk.\n\n## Why read telemetry after teardown via `home_root` scan\n\n`HomeIsolation.teardown(keep=True)` preserves the directory but clears\nthe `_home_path` slot, so the post-run inspection can't ask the\ninstance \"what was your home_path?\". Two options:\n\n1. **Capture `home_path` during the worker run** and store it in the\n   record alongside `session_id` etc. This is what\n   `record[\"home_path\"]` already does, so most tests use this path.\n2. **Scan `home_root` for `{eval_id}-*` dirs** to verify the\n   `from-the-outside` view that mkdtemp's `prefix=` did the right thing.\n   Helpers `_read_telemetry_event` / `_read_telemetry_home` use this so\n   the namespace assertions don't depend on the worker's bookkeeping.\n\nBoth views are checked. They agree iff the prefix mkdtemp contract holds.\n\n## Why three views of `session_id` (record, JSONL, env)\n\nThe contract is \"each eval owns its own session_id.\" A passing test\nthat reads only one view (e.g. the worker's record) wouldn't catch a\nregression where `env()` returns a stale value while the record looks\nright. So `test_each_eval_receives_unique_session_id` checks:\n\n- `record[\"session_id\"]` — what the worker captured at `env()` time.\n- JSONL's `session_id` field — what was written to disk.\n- `iso.env()[\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"]` — what a downstream caller would\n  read *after* the run, before teardown clears the slot.\n\nWait — teardown clears the slot. The test therefore re-creates\n`HomeIsolation` with the same args and calls `env()` post-run, which\nreturns the same `session_id` because `session_id` is a constructor\nfield, not a mkdtemp output. The point is to prove that the env() view\nmatches the disk view, which it does.\n\n(An earlier draft tried to read `env()` on the original instance, which\nraised `RuntimeError` after teardown. The current shape correctly\ntreats `session_id` as a deterministic input and verifies all three\nviews agree.)\n\n## Why two clamp tests instead of one\n\n`TestParallelClamp::test_parallel_above_max_clamps_to_fifteen` in\nT03.15 already proves the clamp downward. What that test doesn't\nprove is that the clamp is **not over-restrictive** — e.g. a regression\nthat clamped everything to 10 would still pass that test (≤15 stays\n≤15). `test_parallel_at_max_admits_fifteen_concurrent_workers` closes\nthat gap: with parallel=15 and 15 specs holding a barrier, exactly 15\nmust be in flight simultaneously. Together the two clamp tests pin both\nends of the NFR-PERF2 contract.\n\n## Concurrency barrier design\n\nNaïve \"count active workers\" measurements race: by the time you read\n`len(active)`, an earlier worker may have decremented. The pattern\nused here:\n\n```\nenter() {\n  with lock: active.append(spec)\n  with lock: observed_concurrency.append(len(active))\n  if hold_event: hold_event.wait()\n  with lock: active.remove(spec)\n}\n```\n\nThe snapshot is taken **under the lock**, immediately after appending,\nso `max(observed_concurrency)` accurately reflects the peak. Combined\nwith a `threading.Event` barrier released by a `threading.Timer(1.0,\n...)`, workers actually pile up before any can return — which is what\nmakes the peak meaningful.\n\nFor the unbounded (no-barrier) test, the suite is fast enough that\nconcurrency assertions are bounded by ≤8 only (not exactly 8), since\nall 15 finish in ~0.1s and overlap depends on scheduler timing. That\nflake-resistance is intentional: the *clamp* matters, not the\narrival-rate-derived ceiling.\n\n## Out-of-scope items deliberately not implemented\n\n- **Hook deployment per HOME.** T03.04 owns the deploy; the worker\n  here writes the telemetry directly to keep the test focused on the\n  isolation surface FR-G2 names.\n- **PTY/process launch.** Phase 4 wires `ClaudeProcessAdapter` into the\n  real worker; T03.16 demonstrates the orchestrator+isolation\n  composition that adapter will plug into.\n- **Time-isolation (`time_offset_sec`).** Covered in\n  `test_home_isolation.py` for the unit surface; not in FR-G2's\n  scope.\n- **Cancellation under load.** T03.15's cancellation tests cover the\n  scheduler's cancel semantics; mixing cancellation into a 15-wide\n  integration test would dilute both signals.\n\n## Verification evidence\n\nSee `evidence.md` in this directory. Summary:\n- 7/7 new tests pass in 2.21s.\n- 47/47 regression tests pass across orchestrator + isolation in 0.50s.\n- 54/54 combined (new + regression) pass in 2.58s.\n","numLines":153,"startLine":1,"totalLines":153}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01E1Re1Ai2rMt7ajZ4s16yo1","type":"tool_result","content":"120\t# exported so :class:`EvalRunner` (T03.x) and forensics tooling can locate the\n121\t# tag without re-deriving the path.\n122\tSETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH = \".eval-meta/setup_failed\"\n123\t\n124\t\n125\tdef _write_setup_failed_tag(home_path: Path, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n126\t    \"\"\"Write the NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` artifact tag under ``home_path``.\n127\t\n128\t    The tag distinguishes \"harness bug or environmental refusal during\n129\t    setup\" (status ERRORED) from \"eval ran and produced a failing\n130\t    assertion\" (status FAIL). The contents are split for grep-ability:\n131\t\n132\t    * Line 1 is the exception class name verbatim\n133\t      (``type(exc).__name__``), so a downstream EvalRunner can bucket\n134\t      the error with a single ``head -n1`` read.\n135\t    * The remainder is the formatted traceback for forensic inspection.\n136\t\n137\t    The helper is best-effort: ``setup`` catches and discards any\n138\t    exception this function raises so a write failure (e.g. the\n139\t    ``.eval-meta`` directory cannot be created because the per-eval HOME\n140\t    was made read-only by a future hardening step) never masks the\n141\t    original setup exception.\n142\t\n143\t    Args:\n144\t        home_path: The per-eval HOME ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n145\t            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. The tag is materialized at\n146\t            ``home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH``.\n147\t        exc: The exception that interrupted ``setup``. Used only to\n148\t            populate the tag contents; this helper never re-raises it.\n149\t    \"\"\"\n150\t\n151\t    tag_path = home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\n152\t    tag_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n153\t    # ``traceback.format_exception`` reads the active exception state when\n154\t    # the helper is invoked from inside an ``except`` clause. Pin the\n155\t    # arguments explicitly so the helper also works when called outside\n156\t    # one (defensive — the only call site is currently inside ``except``).\n157\t    tb_lines = traceback.format_exception(type(exc), exc, exc.__traceback__)\n158\t    payload = f\"{type(exc).__name__}\\n\" + \"\".join(tb_lines)\n159\t    tag_path.write_text(payload, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n160\t\n161\t\n162\tclass HomeContainmentViolation(Exception):\n163\t    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`containment_guard` when any FR-ISO2 check fails.\n164\t\n165\t    The exception is the single hard-failure surface of FR-ISO2 — it is\n166\t    the only thing :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` may raise once\n167\t    :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` has succeeded and BEFORE the hook adapter\n168\t    (T02.14) writes a single byte under the per-eval HOME. Catching this\n169\t    type at the orchestrator level is sufficient to cover all three\n170\t    containment checks (eval_id regex, scratch-root allowlist, post-\n171\t    mkdtemp symlink resolution); callers MUST NOT branch on the\n172\t    underlying :class:`InvalidEvalId` / :class:`ScratchRootViolation`\n173\t    causes — they are exposed via ``__cause__`` for forensics only.\n174\t\n175\t    The exception MUST raise before any hook deploy or eval-state\n176\t    write so NFR-SEC2 (\"hard refusal before side effects\") is upheld;\n177\t    the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is responsible for\n178\t    preserving the partial per-eval HOME with a ``setup_failed`` tag\n179\t    once it lands.\n180\t\n181\t    Attributes:\n182\t        check: Short identifier for the failed check (``\"eval_id\"``,\n183\t            ``\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``, ``\"home_path_resolution\"`` or\n184\t            ``\"home_path_escape\"``). Lets reporters bucket failures\n185\t            without parsing the human-readable message.\n186\t        home_path: The per-eval HOME path the guard was asked to\n187\t            validate. Stored verbatim as supplied by the caller (no\n188\t            resolution) so forensics keep the original input.\n189\t        scratch_root: The declared scratch root the guard checked\n190\t            ``home_path`` against. Stored verbatim.\n191\t        eval_id: The eval id under inspection. Stored verbatim\n192\t            (``repr``-rendered) so non-string smuggled scalars are\n193\t            visible in reporter output.\n194\t        detail: Free-form one-line message describing the specific\n195\t            failure. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01NntFK5gqFfrimQbZ3u1qrr","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01VLupvFsiewi7bQcaoVovG6","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/tests/cli/eval/test_no_shared_state.py","content":"\"\"\"NFR-ISO1 integration test: no shared mutable state across N×15 trials.\n\nTask T03.20 / Deliverable D-0061 / Roadmap row R-061.\n\nThis file owns the *cross-trial* NFR-ISO1 acceptance the design-spec pins:\n\n    \"No shared HOMEs, no shared file handles (e.g. ``auggie-first.jsonl``),\n    and no port collisions at ``--parallel 15``. Tests run N×15 trials.\"\n\nWhere ``test_parallel_15.py`` (T03.16 / D-0058) exercises **one** parallel\nrun of 15 evals and confirms intra-run isolation, this file iterates the\nsame composition **3 trials in a row** against a shared scratch root and\nconfirms inter-run isolation. That arrangement is the only one that can\nexpose low-probability race conditions on long-lived state — e.g. a\nworker that accidentally caches a HOME path in a module-level dict, a\nshared JSONL file handle held across runs, or a session_id allocator\nthat resets to a deterministic seed at run boundaries. A single trial\ncannot catch those bugs; 3 × 15 == 45 evals against the same scratch\nroot can.\n\nWhat the test asserts pairwise across **all 45 evals**:\n\n1. **HOME path uniqueness.** Every eval's :attr:`HomeIsolation.home_path`\n   is distinct from every other eval's, regardless of trial. The\n   ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` randomness inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`\n   is what guarantees this even across trials — the assertion fails\n   immediately if a regression starts re-using a HOME root.\n2. **session_id uniqueness.** Each eval's ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is\n   distinct across all trials. The worker allocates the id from a\n   ``(trial, spec.id)`` tuple so a regression that re-uses a previous\n   trial's id (e.g. a UUID generator seeded once per process) would\n   surface as a collision.\n3. **Per-eval JSONL path uniqueness.** The per-eval\n   ``home_path/.eval-logs/telemetry.jsonl`` path is distinct across all\n   45 evals. This is the proxy for the AC's \"no shared file handles\n   (e.g. ``auggie-first.jsonl``)\" — once two evals diverge on JSONL\n   path, no shared write handle can fan out into the wrong namespace.\n4. **No port collisions.** :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` is inspected for\n   any ``*_PORT`` / ``*PORT*`` keys; the harness's contract is that\n   no port-bound state leaks into the per-eval env. A regression that\n   added a port variable to the isolation layer would be caught here\n   even before the harness binds the port — the variable's mere\n   presence is the smell.\n5. **No telemetry cross-talk.** Each per-eval JSONL is parsed and the\n   embedded ``eval_id`` / ``session_id`` / ``trial`` fields match the\n   eval that owns the file. Two evals that ended up writing to the\n   same file would surface here as \"eval ``E007`` reads ``E003`` in\n   its own JSONL,\" which is a stronger assertion than path\n   uniqueness alone (path uniqueness rules out shared *paths*; this\n   rules out shared *content* even when the paths happen to differ).\n\nThe worker stub deliberately mirrors the shape used by\n``test_parallel_15.py``: a real :class:`HomeIsolation`, a real\n:meth:`setup`/``env``/``teardown`` cycle, a real JSONL write under the\neval-private namespace, and a synthetic PASS outcome. The Phase 4 PTY\nadapter is out of scope; NFR-ISO1 is a statement about the\norchestrator+isolation composition, not about the subprocess that runs\nunder the per-eval HOME.\n\nThe number of trials is sized to be high enough to catch low-probability\nraces (3 trials × 15 evals × ~8 simultaneous workers gives many\ndistinct interleavings under the GIL) while staying cheap enough to run\nin well under a second on the dev host.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport threading\nfrom collections import Counter\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.config import EvalConfig\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.isolation import HomeIsolation\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.orchestrator import RunOrchestrator\n\n# Number of trial repetitions. NFR-ISO1's \"N×15 trials\" leaves N to\n# implementations; 3 is the smallest value that meaningfully separates\n# \"happened to work once\" from \"the contract holds run-over-run\" while\n# keeping the wall-clock cost trivial.\nN_TRIALS = 3\nEVALS_PER_TRIAL = 15\nPARALLEL = 8\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Fixtures\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef scratch_root(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"A *single* scratch root shared across every trial.\n\n    Sharing the root is the entire point: if two trials independently\n    materialize HOMEs under the same directory and the runner caches\n    state at module scope, the second trial's evals will collide with\n    the first's. A per-trial scratch root would mask that bug.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    root = tmp_path / \"eval-runs\"\n    root.mkdir()\n    return root\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef eval_config(scratch_root: Path) -> EvalConfig:\n    \"\"\":class:`EvalConfig` whose allowlist contains the shared scratch root.\"\"\"\n\n    return EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(scratch_root,))\n\n\ndef _build_specs(count: int) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n    \"\"\"Build ``count`` deterministically-named eval specs.\n\n    Ids use ``E000``..``E0NN`` zero-padded so the FR-SCH2 regex\n    (``^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$``) is satisfied. The spec list is\n    identical across trials *on purpose* — re-using the same ids\n    across trials is what proves the runner does not key any cached\n    state off eval_id (a regression that did so would surface as\n    cross-trial collisions on either HOME or telemetry paths).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return [EvalSpec(id=f\"E{i:03d}\", title=f\"eval-{i}\") for i in range(count)]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Real-isolation worker (mirrors test_parallel_15.py but stamps a trial id)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _make_isolation_worker(\n    *,\n    trial: int,\n    scratch_root: Path,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n    records: list[dict[str, Any]],\n    records_lock: threading.Lock,\n):\n    \"\"\"Build a worker that captures per-eval state into a thread-safe list.\n\n    Each captured record contains everything the test needs to assert\n    cross-trial uniqueness: the trial id, the eval id, the materialized\n    ``home_path``, the allocated ``session_id``, the env dict the\n    worker would have handed to the subprocess, and the JSONL path the\n    worker wrote into. Records survive the trial boundary because the\n    worker uses ``teardown(keep=True)`` — the HOMEs stay on disk so the\n    final assertions can re-read them.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def worker(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        # Allocate a session_id that bakes in both the trial number and\n        # the eval id. If a future regression introduced a UUID\n        # generator that reset its seed at run boundaries, the test\n        # would still pass on session_id uniqueness (because the worker\n        # holds the canonical mapping) — but the env() / JSONL views\n        # would diverge, which Test 5 catches.\n        session_id = f\"sess-t{trial}-{spec.id}\"\n        isolation = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=session_id,\n        )\n        home_path = isolation.setup(config=config)\n        env = dict(isolation.env())\n\n        # Write a per-eval JSONL log under the eval-private namespace.\n        # The path is identical in shape to the one ``test_parallel_15``\n        # writes, so a cross-trial regression that shared a file\n        # handle would manifest as either a path collision (Test 3)\n        # or duplicated content across files (Test 5).\n        telemetry_dir = home_path / \".eval-logs\"\n        telemetry_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        telemetry_path = telemetry_dir / \"telemetry.jsonl\"\n        event = {\n            \"event\": \"eval_started\",\n            \"trial\": trial,\n            \"eval_id\": spec.id,\n            \"home_path\": str(home_path),\n            \"session_id\": session_id,\n            \"env_home\": env[\"HOME\"],\n            \"env_session_id\": env[\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"],\n            \"telemetry_namespace\": str(telemetry_path),\n        }\n        telemetry_path.write_text(\n            json.dumps(event, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        )\n\n        with records_lock:\n            records.append(\n                {\n                    \"trial\": trial,\n                    \"eval_id\": spec.id,\n                    \"home_path\": str(home_path),\n                    \"session_id\": session_id,\n                    \"env\": env,\n                    \"telemetry_path\": str(telemetry_path),\n                }\n            )\n\n        # Keep the HOME on disk so the final assertions can re-read the\n        # JSONL after every trial has finished.\n        isolation.teardown(keep=True)\n\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"PASS\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={\"telemetry\": str(telemetry_path)},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    return worker\n\n\ndef _run_trials(\n    scratch_root: Path,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n    *,\n    n_trials: int = N_TRIALS,\n    evals_per_trial: int = EVALS_PER_TRIAL,\n    parallel: int = PARALLEL,\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Execute ``n_trials`` of ``evals_per_trial``-eval parallel runs.\n\n    Returns the consolidated record list, ordered by trial then by\n    completion (the per-trial order is non-deterministic under\n    ``as_completed``, which is exactly what cross-trial uniqueness\n    assertions want to ride on top of).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    records: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    records_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n    for trial in range(n_trials):\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            trial=trial,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=config,\n            records=records,\n            records_lock=records_lock,\n        )\n        specs = _build_specs(evals_per_trial)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n        outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=parallel)\n        # Sanity: the trial itself must succeed before cross-trial\n        # assertions can be meaningful. A failure here means the\n        # orchestrator/isolation composition broke before NFR-ISO1\n        # could be evaluated.\n        assert len(outcomes) == evals_per_trial, (\n            f\"trial {trial}: expected {evals_per_trial} outcomes, \"\n            f\"got {len(outcomes)}\"\n        )\n        assert all(o.status == \"PASS\" for o in outcomes), (\n            f\"trial {trial}: non-PASS outcomes: \"\n            f\"{[(o.eval_id, o.status) for o in outcomes if o.status != 'PASS']}\"\n        )\n\n    return records\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# NFR-ISO1 cross-trial assertions\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestNoSharedStateAcrossTrials:\n    \"\"\"The NFR-ISO1 cross-trial acceptance bundle.\n\n    Every test re-runs the 3×15 trial composition and asserts a distinct\n    slice of the no-shared-state contract. Splitting the assertions\n    keeps a failure pinpointed to the broken guarantee rather than\n    bundling everything into one opaque assert.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_three_trials_each_with_fifteen_evals_all_pass(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Pre-condition: the trials themselves succeed.\"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        assert len(records) == N_TRIALS * EVALS_PER_TRIAL\n\n    def test_home_paths_pairwise_distinct_across_all_trials(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"No two evals — across any trial — see the same HOME.\"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        home_paths = [r[\"home_path\"] for r in records]\n        duplicates = [\n            path for path, count in Counter(home_paths).items() if count > 1\n        ]\n        assert not duplicates, (\n            f\"shared HOME paths detected across trials: {duplicates!r}\"\n        )\n        assert len(set(home_paths)) == len(records)\n\n    def test_home_paths_all_under_shared_scratch_root(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Every materialized HOME is rooted under the declared scratch root.\n\n        Uniqueness alone is not enough — a regression that ``mkdtemp``-ed\n        outside the scratch root would *also* yield unique paths but\n        violate FR-ISO2. Re-asserting containment here closes that gap\n        for the cross-trial case.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        scratch_resolved = scratch_root.resolve()\n        for record in records:\n            home_path = Path(record[\"home_path\"]).resolve()\n            assert home_path.is_relative_to(scratch_resolved), (\n                f\"trial {record['trial']} eval {record['eval_id']}: \"\n                f\"home {home_path} escapes scratch root {scratch_resolved}\"\n            )\n\n    def test_session_ids_pairwise_distinct_across_all_trials(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is distinct across every eval, every trial.\n\n        The session id is the linchpin for any downstream telemetry\n        join — if two evals share a session id, their hook logs collide\n        in the eyes of the consumer even if their HOMEs are different.\n        Verified in two views simultaneously: the worker's canonical\n        record AND the env dict the subprocess would have observed.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        session_ids = [r[\"session_id\"] for r in records]\n        env_session_ids = [r[\"env\"][\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"] for r in records]\n\n        assert len(set(session_ids)) == len(records), (\n            f\"shared session_ids in canonical record: \"\n            f\"{[sid for sid, c in Counter(session_ids).items() if c > 1]!r}\"\n        )\n        assert len(set(env_session_ids)) == len(records), (\n            f\"shared session_ids in env dict: \"\n            f\"{[sid for sid, c in Counter(env_session_ids).items() if c > 1]!r}\"\n        )\n        # The two views agree pairwise: a regression that allocated a\n        # unique session_id but then exported a stale value via env()\n        # would not be caught by either uniqueness check alone.\n        assert session_ids == env_session_ids\n\n    def test_telemetry_paths_pairwise_distinct_across_all_trials(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"JSONL paths are pairwise distinct across all 45 evals.\n\n        Proxy for \"no shared file handles (e.g. ``auggie-first.jsonl``)\":\n        once the paths diverge, no shared write handle can fan out into\n        the wrong namespace by accident.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        telemetry_paths = [r[\"telemetry_path\"] for r in records]\n        duplicates = [\n            path\n            for path, count in Counter(telemetry_paths).items()\n            if count > 1\n        ]\n        assert not duplicates, (\n            f\"shared telemetry paths detected across trials: {duplicates!r}\"\n        )\n        assert len(set(telemetry_paths)) == len(records)\n\n    def test_telemetry_contents_belong_to_owning_eval(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Each JSONL file's contents match the eval that owns the path.\n\n        Stronger than path uniqueness: even if two workers happened to\n        produce distinct paths, a shared file handle could write the\n        wrong record into one of them. Reading the file back and\n        matching ``eval_id`` / ``session_id`` / ``trial`` rules that\n        regression out.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        for record in records:\n            telemetry_path = Path(record[\"telemetry_path\"])\n            assert telemetry_path.exists(), (\n                f\"trial {record['trial']} eval {record['eval_id']}: \"\n                f\"telemetry missing at {telemetry_path}\"\n            )\n            with telemetry_path.open(encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n                event = json.loads(fh.readline())\n            assert event[\"trial\"] == record[\"trial\"], (\n                f\"cross-talk: telemetry at {telemetry_path} carries \"\n                f\"trial {event['trial']!r}; record says {record['trial']!r}\"\n            )\n            assert event[\"eval_id\"] == record[\"eval_id\"], (\n                f\"cross-talk: telemetry at {telemetry_path} carries \"\n                f\"eval_id {event['eval_id']!r}; record says \"\n                f\"{record['eval_id']!r}\"\n            )\n            assert event[\"session_id\"] == record[\"session_id\"]\n            assert event[\"env_session_id\"] == record[\"env\"][\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"]\n            assert event[\"env_home\"] == record[\"env\"][\"HOME\"]\n            assert event[\"telemetry_namespace\"] == record[\"telemetry_path\"]\n\n    def test_no_port_state_leaks_into_per_eval_env(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"No port-bound variable appears in any per-eval env.\n\n        NFR-ISO1 names \"no port collisions\" alongside HOME and JSONL.\n        :class:`HomeIsolation` does not bind any port today, so the\n        cleanest assertion is the structural one: no variable in the\n        per-eval env dict carries a ``PORT`` suffix or ``_PORT_``\n        infix. A regression that added one would have to opt in\n        explicitly, and would surface here before any actual port\n        could be claimed.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        for record in records:\n            offending = [\n                key\n                for key in record[\"env\"]\n                if \"PORT\" in key.upper()\n            ]\n            assert not offending, (\n                f\"trial {record['trial']} eval {record['eval_id']}: \"\n                f\"unexpected port-bearing env keys {offending!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def test_env_home_matches_recorded_home_path_for_every_eval(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``env()['HOME']`` is the same string the worker recorded.\n\n        Catches a regression where :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` returns a\n        stale HOME from a previous trial — e.g. because the env-dict\n        was memoized at module scope. Tests run N×15 trials.\"\n\nWhere ``test_parallel_15.py`` (T03.16 / D-0058) exercises **one** parallel\nrun of 15 evals and confirms intra-run isolation, this file iterates the\nsame composition **3 trials in a row** against a shared scratch root and\nconfirms inter-run isolation. That arrangement is the only one that can\nexpose low-probability race conditions on long-lived state — e.g. a\nworker that accidentally caches a HOME path in a module-level dict, a\nshared JSONL file handle held across runs, or a session_id allocator\nthat resets to a deterministic seed at run boundaries. A single trial\ncannot catch those bugs; 3 × 15 == 45 evals against the same scratch\nroot can.\n\nWhat the test asserts pairwise across **all 45 evals**:\n\n1. **HOME path uniqueness.** Every eval's :attr:`HomeIsolation.home_path`\n   is distinct from every other eval's, regardless of trial. The\n   ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` randomness inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`\n   is what guarantees this even across trials — the assertion fails\n   immediately if a regression starts re-using a HOME root.\n2. **session_id uniqueness.** Each eval's ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is\n   distinct across all trials. This is the proxy for the AC's \"no shared file handles\n   (e.g. ``auggie-first.jsonl``)\" — once two evals diverge on JSONL\n   path, no shared write handle can fan out into the wrong namespace.\n4. **No port collisions.** :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` is inspected for\n   any ``*_PORT`` / ``*PORT*`` keys; the harness's contract is that\n   no port-bound state leaks into the per-eval env. If a future regression introduced a UUID\n        # generator that reset its seed at run boundaries, the test\n        # would still pass on session_id uniqueness (because the worker\n        # holds the canonical mapping) — but the env() / JSONL views\n        # would diverge, which Test 5 catches.\n        session_id = f\"sess-t{trial}-{spec.id}\"\n        isolation = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=session_id,\n        )\n        home_path = isolation.setup(config=config)\n        env = dict(isolation.env())\n\n        # Write a per-eval JSONL log under the eval-private namespace.\n        # The path is identical in shape to the one ``test_parallel_15``\n        # writes, so a cross-trial regression that shared a file\n        # handle would manifest as either a path collision (Test 3)\n        # or duplicated content across files (Test 5).\n        telemetry_dir = home_path / \".eval-logs\"\n        telemetry_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        telemetry_path = telemetry_dir / \"telemetry.jsonl\"\n        event = {\n            \"event\": \"eval_started\",\n            \"trial\": trial,\n            \"eval_id\": spec.id,\n            \"home_path\": str(home_path),\n            \"session_id\": session_id,\n            \"env_home\": env[\"HOME\"],\n            \"env_session_id\": env[\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"],\n            \"telemetry_namespace\": str(telemetry_path),\n        }\n        telemetry_path.write_text(\n            json.dumps(event, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        )\n\n        with records_lock:\n            records.append(\n                {\n                    \"trial\": trial,\n                    \"eval_id\": spec.id,\n                    \"home_path\": str(home_path),\n                    \"session_id\": session_id,\n                    \"env\": env,\n                    \"telemetry_path\": str(telemetry_path),\n                }\n            )\n\n        # Keep the HOME on disk so the final assertions can re-read the\n        # JSONL after every trial has finished.\n        isolation.teardown(keep=True)\n\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"PASS\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={\"telemetry\": str(telemetry_path)},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    return worker\n\n\ndef _run_trials(\n    scratch_root: Path,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n    *,\n    n_trials: int = N_TRIALS,\n    evals_per_trial: int = EVALS_PER_TRIAL,\n    parallel: int = PARALLEL,\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Execute ``n_trials`` of ``evals_per_trial``-eval parallel runs.\n\n    Returns the consolidated record list, ordered by trial then by\n    completion (the per-trial order is non-deterministic under\n    ``as_completed``, which is exactly what cross-trial uniqueness\n    assertions want to ride on top of).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    records: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    records_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n    for trial in range(n_trials):\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            trial=trial,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=config,\n            records=records,\n            records_lock=records_lock,\n        )\n        specs = _build_specs(evals_per_trial)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n        outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=parallel)\n        # Sanity: the trial itself must succeed before cross-trial\n        # assertions can be meaningful. A failure here means the\n        # orchestrator/isolation composition broke before NFR-ISO1\n        # could be evaluated.\n        assert len(outcomes) == evals_per_trial, (\n            f\"trial {trial}: expected {evals_per_trial} outcomes, \"\n            f\"got {len(outcomes)}\"\n        )\n        assert all(o.status == \"PASS\" for o in outcomes), (\n            f\"trial {trial}: non-PASS outcomes: \"\n            f\"{[(o.eval_id, o.status) for o in outcomes if o.status != 'PASS']}\"\n        )\n\n    return records\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# NFR-ISO1 cross-trial assertions\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestNoSharedStateAcrossTrials:\n    \"\"\"The NFR-ISO1 cross-trial acceptance bundle.\n\n    Every test re-runs the 3×15 trial composition and asserts a distinct\n    slice of the no-shared-state contract. Splitting the assertions\n    keeps a failure pinpointed to the broken guarantee rather than\n    bundling everything into one opaque assert.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_three_trials_each_with_fifteen_evals_all_pass(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Pre-condition: the trials themselves succeed.\"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        assert len(records) == N_TRIALS * EVALS_PER_TRIAL\n\n    def test_home_paths_pairwise_distinct_across_all_trials(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"No two evals — across any trial — see the same HOME.\"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        home_paths = [r[\"home_path\"] for r in records]\n        duplicates = [\n            path for path, count in Counter(home_paths).items() if count > 1\n        ]\n        assert not duplicates, (\n            f\"shared HOME paths detected across trials: {duplicates!r}\"\n        )\n        assert len(set(home_paths)) == len(records)\n\n    def test_home_paths_all_under_shared_scratch_root(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Every materialized HOME is rooted under the declared scratch root.\n\n        Uniqueness alone is not enough — a regression that ``mkdtemp``-ed\n        outside the scratch root would *also* yield unique paths but\n        violate FR-ISO2. Re-asserting containment here closes that gap\n        for the cross-trial case.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        scratch_resolved = scratch_root.resolve()\n        for record in records:\n            home_path = Path(record[\"home_path\"]).resolve()\n            assert home_path.is_relative_to(scratch_resolved), (\n                f\"trial {record['trial']} eval {record['eval_id']}: \"\n                f\"home {home_path} escapes scratch root {scratch_resolved}\"\n            )\n\n    def test_session_ids_pairwise_distinct_across_all_trials(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is distinct across every eval, every trial.\n\n        The session id is the linchpin for any downstream telemetry\n        join — if two evals share a session id, their hook logs collide\n        in the eyes of the consumer even if their HOMEs are different.\n        Verified in two views simultaneously: the worker's canonical\n        record AND the env dict the subprocess would have observed.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        session_ids = [r[\"session_id\"] for r in records]\n        env_session_ids = [r[\"env\"][\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"] for r in records]\n\n        assert len(set(session_ids)) == len(records), (\n            f\"shared session_ids in canonical record: \"\n            f\"{[sid for sid, c in Counter(session_ids).items() if c > 1]!r}\"\n        )\n        assert len(set(env_session_ids)) == len(records), (\n            f\"shared session_ids in env dict: \"\n            f\"{[sid for sid, c in Counter(env_session_ids).items() if c > 1]!r}\"\n        )\n        # The two views agree pairwise: a regression that allocated a\n        # unique session_id but then exported a stale value via env()\n        # would not be caught by either uniqueness check alone.\n        assert session_ids == env_session_ids\n\n    def test_telemetry_paths_pairwise_distinct_across_all_trials(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"JSONL paths are pairwise distinct across all 45 evals.\n\n        Proxy for \"no shared file handles (e.g. ``auggie-first.jsonl``)\":\n        once the paths diverge, no shared write handle can fan out into\n        the wrong namespace by accident.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        telemetry_paths = [r[\"telemetry_path\"] for r in records]\n        duplicates = [\n            path\n            for path, count in Counter(telemetry_paths).items()\n            if count > 1\n        ]\n        assert not duplicates, (\n            f\"shared telemetry paths detected across trials: {duplicates!r}\"\n        )\n        assert len(set(telemetry_paths)) == len(records)\n\n    def test_telemetry_contents_belong_to_owning_eval(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Each JSONL file's contents match the eval that owns the path.\n\n        Stronger than path uniqueness: even if two workers happened to\n        produce distinct paths, a shared file handle could write the\n        wrong record into one of them. Reading the file back and\n        matching ``eval_id`` / ``session_id`` / ``trial`` rules that\n        regression out.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        for record in records:\n            telemetry_path = Path(record[\"telemetry_path\"])\n            assert telemetry_path.exists(), (\n                f\"trial {record['trial']} eval {record['eval_id']}: \"\n                f\"telemetry missing at {telemetry_path}\"\n            )\n            with telemetry_path.open(encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n                event = json.loads(fh.readline())\n            assert event[\"trial\"] == record[\"trial\"], (\n                f\"cross-talk: telemetry at {telemetry_path} carries \"\n                f\"trial {event['trial']!r}; record says {record['trial']!r}\"\n            )\n            assert event[\"eval_id\"] == record[\"eval_id\"], (\n                f\"cross-talk: telemetry at {telemetry_path} carries \"\n                f\"eval_id {event['eval_id']!r}; record says \"\n                f\"{record['eval_id']!r}\"\n            )\n            assert event[\"session_id\"] == record[\"session_id\"]\n            assert event[\"env_session_id\"] == record[\"env\"][\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"]\n            assert event[\"env_home\"] == record[\"env\"][\"HOME\"]\n            assert event[\"telemetry_namespace\"] == record[\"telemetry_path\"]\n\n    def test_no_port_state_leaks_into_per_eval_env(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"No port-bound variable appears in any per-eval env.\n\n        NFR-ISO1 names \"no port collisions\" alongside HOME and JSONL.\n        :class:`HomeIsolation` does not bind any port today, so the\n        cleanest assertion is the structural one: no variable in the\n        per-eval env dict carries a ``PORT`` suffix or ``_PORT_``\n        infix. A regression that added one would have to opt in\n        explicitly, and would surface here before any actual port\n        could be claimed.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        for record in records:\n            offending = [\n                key\n                for key in record[\"env\"]\n                if \"PORT\" in key.upper()\n            ]\n            assert not offending, (\n                f\"trial {record['trial']} eval {record['eval_id']}: \"\n                f\"unexpected port-bearing env keys {offending!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def test_env_home_matches_recorded_home_path_for_every_eval(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``env()['HOME']`` is the same string the worker recorded.\n\n        Catches a regression where :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` returns a\n        stale HOME from a previous trial — e.g. because the env-dict\n        was memoized at module scope. The assertion is per-eval, so a\n        single drift surfaces with the offending eval id rather than\n        as a generic count mismatch.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        for record in records:\n            assert record[\"env\"][\"HOME\"] == record[\"home_path\"], (\n                f\"trial {record['trial']} eval {record['eval_id']}: \"\n                f\"env HOME ({record['env']['HOME']!r}) does not match \"\n                f\"recorded home_path ({record['home_path']!r}); a stale \"\n                f\"env dict has leaked across the trial boundary\"\n            )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# NFR-ISO1 intra-trial sanity (cheap regression guard)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestNoSharedStateWithinEachTrial:\n    \"\"\"A single-trial slice of NFR-ISO1, kept separate so a failure here\n    pinpoints \"broken intra-trial\" vs \"broken cross-trial\" without\n    requiring the reader to diff the two suites.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_each_trial_yields_fifteen_unique_homes(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Within any one trial, all 15 HOMEs are pairwise distinct.\"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        for trial in range(N_TRIALS):\n            trial_records = [r for r in records if r[\"trial\"] == trial]\n            assert len(trial_records) == EVALS_PER_TRIAL\n            homes = {r[\"home_path\"] for r in trial_records}\n            assert len(homes) == EVALS_PER_TRIAL, (\n                f\"trial {trial}: only {len(homes)} unique HOMEs from \"\n                f\"{EVALS_PER_TRIAL} evals\"\n            )\n\n    def test_each_trial_yields_fifteen_unique_session_ids(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Within any one trial, all 15 session_ids are pairwise distinct.\"\"\"\n\n        records = _run_trials(scratch_root, eval_config)\n        for trial in range(N_TRIALS):\n            trial_records = [r for r in records if r[\"trial\"] == trial]\n            session_ids = {r[\"session_id\"] for r in trial_records}\n            assert len(session_ids) == EVALS_PER_TRIAL\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

Tests run N×15 trials.\"\n\n## Purpose\n\nT03.16 (D-0058) already proves that a *single* parallel run of 15 evals\nyields per-eval isolated HOMEs, session_ids, and telemetry namespaces.\nWhat T03.16 cannot detect is a class of regressions that only surface\n*across* runs:\n\n- A module-level dict that caches `mkdtemp` outputs by `eval_id` and\n  returns the stale path on the next trial.\n- A UUID generator seeded once per process so trial 1's session_ids\n  re-appear in trial 2.\n- A JSONL writer that holds a file handle open across runs and fans new\n  writes into the previous trial's file.\n- A future port allocator that binds at `setup` time and re-binds the\n  same port in the next trial because the registry is never cleared.\n\nT03.20 closes that gap by iterating the same composition `N=3` times\nagainst a **single shared scratch root** and asserting cross-trial\nuniqueness pairwise across all `3 × 15 = 45` evals.\n\n## Integration scenario\n\n### Wiring\n\n```\ntrial in 0..N-1:\n    spec list (15)\n        │\n        └─→ RunOrchestrator(run_one=isolation_worker_t{trial}, cancellation_token=None)\n                  │\n                  └─→ ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=clamp(parallel=8, [1,15]))\n                          │\n                          └─→ isolation_worker(spec):\n                                  HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id,\n                                                home_root=SHARED_scratch_root,\n                                                session_id=f\"sess-t{trial}-{spec.id}\")\n                                      │\n                                      ├─ setup(config=permissive_config)\n                                      │     → mkdtemp({eval_id}-…) under home_root\n                                      ├─ env()\n                                      │     → {HOME, CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}\n EvalOutcome(status=\"PASS\", …,\n                                                     artifacts={\"telemetry\": …})\n        ↓\n        records list (thread-safe append) capturing per-eval state\n```\n\nThe `records` list is the join surface: every worker appends a single\ndict with `trial`, `eval_id`, `home_path`, `session_id`, `env`, and\n`telemetry_path`. After all 3 trials finish, the test reads back the\n45 records and performs the cross-trial assertions.\n\n### Parameters\n\n| Symbol | Value | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| `N_TRIALS` | 3 | Phase 3
